### GHSA-vw2v-g4x7-xcqg — fulgur allows Unbounded string-set value accumulation causes memory/CPU exhaustion (denial of service)
- Aliases: CVE-2026-68539 | Published: 2026-11-25 | Modified: 2026-11-25 | Severity: HIGH
- CWE: CWE-400, CWE-770
- CVSS_V3: `CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:N/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:H`
- Affected crates.io/fulgur: introduced:0.4.4 fixed:0.28.0

`fulgur` converts untrusted HTML/CSS into PDF, commonly on a server that
processes input supplied by many tenants. In versions from 0.4.4 up to (but not
including) the patched release, the CSS `string-set` named-string values
resolved from a document were accumulated per element with no memory budget,
letting a small crafted document amplify into large retained memory (and the CPU
to copy it) — a denial of service.

A `string-set` value is attacker-controlled: it is resolved from an element's
own text (`content(text)`), from one of its attributes (`attr(...)`), or from a
CSS string literal. fulgur accumulated these values per element in two unbounded
places:

1. **Store (default path).** For every element that matches a `string-set` rule,
   fulgur pushes a copy of the resolved value into a store that is retained for
   the whole render and cloned again downstream into a per-node lookup map. This
   happens on the ordinary render path with no opt-in. A single literal reused
   across many elements therefore expands from one copy in the input to N copies
   in memory:

   ```html
   <style>
     p { string-set: title "……a large literal value……" }
   </style>
   <p></p>  <!-- repeated N times -->
   ```

   The store grows to roughly `value_size × N` bytes (and ~3× that across the
   downstream clones) from an input whose large part appears only once — a
   breadth-unbounded amplification.

2. **Per-node snapshot (bookmark / cross-reference path).** When PDF bookmarks
   (`bookmark-label: string(...)`) or CSS `target-*` references are in play,
   fulgur additionally cloned the full running `name → value` map at every
   visited element so `string()` can resolve at that position. One large value
   is then re-cloned at each of N following elements — an `O(N × value_size)`
   retained-memory sink. This is the string counterpart of the counter-snapshot
   sink fixed in
   [GHSA-395p-pj7r-jm42](https://github.com/fulgur-rs/fulgur/security/advisories/GHSA-395p-pj7r-jm42);
   that fix bounded the counter snapshots but did not bound this string twin.

An attacker able to submit HTML/CSS to a fulgur-based conversion service can
trigger memory and CPU exhaustion with a trivially small payload, denying
service to the host and any co-tenants.

## Patches

Fixed in **0.28.0**. The aggregate of each sink is now separately bounded: the
resolved `string-set` store (`MAX_STRING_SET_STORE_BYTES`) and the retained
per-node named-string snapshots (`MAX_STRING_SNAPSHOT_BYTES`). In addition, the
snapshot is no longer recorded for `target-*`-only renders — those snapshots are
consumed only by `bookmark-label: string(...)`, so recording them when no
bookmarks are emitted was both wasted work and the only path that let inline
`target-*` CSS enable the snapshot clone with no bookmark opt-in. The caps are
kept far above any realistic document (short named strings, even thousands of
them); past the cap a `string()` reference degrades to its last recorded value,
which only occurs under adversarial input.

## Workarounds

Upgrade to 0.28.0 or later. If upgrading is not immediately possible, validate
or constrain untrusted CSS — in particular a `string-set` rule with a large
literal (or `content(text)` / `attr(...)`) value matched by many elements —
before passing HTML to fulgur, and bound the size and element count of accepted
input.

## Attack Vector rationale

`fulgur` performs no network I/O of its own; it renders HTML/CSS handed to it by
the embedding application. This advisory scores the crate independent of any
specific adopting program, so per the CVSS v3.1 User Guide §3.7 (scoring library
vulnerabilities related to incoming data) the Attack Vector is assessed as
**Network** for the reasonable worst-case deployment — a network-facing service
that renders untrusted, attacker-supplied HTML without user interaction. A
concrete system that receives the HTML in one component and passes it to fulgur
in a separate component may assess a lower environmental Attack Vector (Local,
per §3.10).
